Including a preface / wherein two little treatises sent to the Author by Mr. Johannes Merckens / Lutheran-Evangelical preacher at Essen: 1. *Christian Instruction on the Freedom to Teach* / and on the scriptural understanding of the **Keys of Binding and Loosing** <note>A theological reference to the authority of the church to forgive or withhold forgiveness for sins.</note>. 2. *Christian Instruction on the Communion of Saints* etc. are summarily repeated / and their impartial grounds of truth are most highly recommended to the modest reader. Octavo. 1704.

**Two Dialogues on Alchemy**, the first of which reveals the genuine meaning of the books of Geber <note>Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, the "father of chemistry," whose medieval works were often written in "gibberish"—deliberately obscured language.</note>, which was intentionally hidden by the author and wrapped in figurative speech; it proves this with certain arguments. Nuremberg, 1674.

Her tomb was an alchemical allegory; an inscription supposedly promised treasure to any king who opened it, but the intruder would find only a warning against greed.</note> was sealed fantastically, not hermetically <note>original: "hermeticè"; refers to the sealed tradition of Hermes Trismegistus.</note>, by an anonymous author: opened by an anonymous author, where, if the Wise Man should inspect it himself, he finds it empty of the promised treasures of kings. Octavo. 1678.

**Minerals; Especially concerning Potable Gold** <note>original: "Auro Potabili"; gold dissolved in a liquid, considered by many alchemists to be a "universal medicine" for prolonging life.</note>, by an Anonymous Hermit. Octavo. <note>original: "8."; a book size where a sheet is folded into eight leaves.</note> New Hanau 1677.

**A brief discourse on the admirable virtues of potable gold** in which are treated the principal foundations of medicine, the origin and cause of all diseases, and which medicines are most suitable for their cure and for the preservation of human health: composed by the Lord of Tourette, recently president of the general masters of the mints of France <note>original: "president des generaux maistres de monnoyes de France"</note>. Dedicated to the Most Christian King. With an Apology for the very useful science of Alchemy, both against those who criticize it, as well as the counterfeiters, thieves, and deceivers who abuse it, by the same Author. Octavo. Lyon, 1575.

**Curious Discourses on the Wonders of Nature**, especially regarding human beings according to their various constitutions; how such things are filled with many remarkable tales, composed in three parts, and collected from the most famous ancient and modern authors for everyone's benefit and pleasure. Quarto. <note>original: "4."; a medium book size.</note> Leipzig 1700.

**Nuremberg Dispensatory** <note>original: "Dispensatorium"; an official book listing medicines and the instructions for their preparation.</note>, folio <note>original: "fol."; the largest standard book format.</note>. See: Cordus <note>Valerius Cordus (1515–1544) authored the first official pharmacopoeia in Germany, the *Dispensatorium*, in 1546.</note>.

--- **[Dispensatory] of Copenhagen** <note>original: "Hafniense"</note>, prepared by order of the superiors by the physicians of Copenhagen, which Thomas Bartholinus published. Quarto. Copenhagen, 1658. See: Bartholinus.

**Medical Dispensatory**, or commentaries on the correct method of preparing medicines currently in use, most useful for physicians and pharmacists <note>original: "Pharmacopœis"</note>, written by Georg Melich, an apothecary of Augsburg in Venice; and translated into the Latin language by Samuel Keller. To which is added a compendium of practical medicine by Francesco Maria de Tectoriis of Urbino. Duodecimo. <note>original: "12."; a small, portable book size.</note> Frankfurt, Palthenian Press, 1601.

**Inquiry concerning Elijah the Artist** <note>original: "Helia Artista"; a legendary figure in alchemical lore, first predicted by Paracelsus, who was expected to appear and reveal the final secrets of transmutation and world transformation.</note>, in which the transformation of metals is clearly and solidly discussed against the opinions of the Jesuits Hagellius and Pererius. Recently added are the Hermetic Canons concerning the spirit, soul, and body of the Greater and Lesser Worlds <note>original: "Majoris & minoris Mundi"; referring to the Macrocosm (the universe) and the Microcosm (the human being).</note>, with an Appendix. Octavo. Marburg, 1608.

**Dorn, Gerard:** *A Bundle of Paracelsian Medicine, ancient and not new, through chemical and medical flowers, collected as if in a concise handbook* <note>original: "Fasciculus Paracelsicæ Medicinæ veteris & non novæ... tanquam in compendiosum promptuarium collectus"</note>, *in which is treated the Life, Death, and Resuscitation of things, the protection and preservation of health, as well as the expulsion of disease through the restoration of natural forces, the preparations of medicines, and their application for all diseases, whether internal or external. Also concerning the generation of a pygmy Homunculus* <note>original: "Hemunculi pygmei"; in alchemy, a "homunculus" is a small, fully formed human being created through artificial, chemical means rather than natural birth.</note> *from the loss of blood’s nourishment.* Quarto. <note>original: "4."; a book size where the paper is folded twice to form four leaves.</note> Frankfurt am Main, 1581.

**By the same author:** *Commentaries on Theophrastus Paracelsus's 'Dawn of the Philosophers,' 'Treasury,' and 'Mineral Economy,' with certain arguments.* Octavo. <note>original: "8."; a book size where the sheet is folded into eight leaves.</note> Frankfurt, 1584.

**By the same author:** *A Collection of Paracelsian Chemistry on the Transmutations of Metals, from all that could be found written by him on these subjects to date. To which is added the Genealogy of Minerals and of all Metals, by the same author.* Octavo. Frankfurt, published by Andreas Wechel, 1581. See also Bernhard regarding chemical miracles <note>original: "Bernhard de myracula chym."; likely referring to the alchemist Bernard of Trevisan.</note>.

*The same author's **Key to Chemical Philosophy**, with which the secret and hidden Dicta and sayings of the Philosophers are opened and resolved. To which the Supernatural Craft* <note>original: "Artificium supernaturale"; a specific alchemical process or work described by Dorn.</note> *has been added along with its related pieces and parts. Translated from Latin into German. Octavo. Strasbourg, 1602.*

**Dorn, Gerard:** *The Key to All Chemical Philosophy, through which the obscure sayings of the philosophers are referenced. A compendium containing three books, partly physical, partly medical, and for the greater part chemical. With 12 copperplate figures.* <note>original: "Figuris æneis"; high-quality engraved illustrations.</note> Duodecimo. <note>original: "12."; a small book size where the sheet is folded into twelve leaves.</note> Lyon, 1567.

**By the same author:** The same, to which is added the *Supernatural Craft*. With copperplate figures. Duodecimo. Frankfurt, 1583. Printed by Christoph Corvinus.

[...nature] and properties of the elements / likewise of thunder / lightning / heat / cold / winds / rain / hail and snow as they manifest in the upper and lower regions / and what causes them. Together with an appendix on the *Quintessence* <note>The "fifth essence," a purified substance beyond the four classical elements (earth, air, fire, water).</note> or 5th essence in all three kingdoms of nature / and a dedication regarding the *Primum Mobile* <note>original: "Primo mobili"; the "first moved" or outermost sphere of the universe in classical astronomy.</note> or first-moving things / as well as Mr. Edmund Halley's <note>The English astronomer famous for Halley's Comet; his inclusion here shows the integration of modern 17th-century science with older alchemical texts.</note> accounts of the winds. Collected together and published by Polycarpus Chrysostomus <note>A pseudonym for the alchemical writer Georg von Welling.</note>. With several figures. *By the same author:* Two Treatises: I. The Nature of the Elements. II. On the Quintessence. To which is added a most worthy-to-read letter regarding the invention of Perpetual Motion <note>original: "Mobilis Pepetui" [sic]; Drebbel was famous for creating a "perpetual motion" clock.</note>. Translated from the Dutch tongue into Latin by Mr. Petrus Laurembergius, Professor at the Hamburg Athenaeum. Duodecimo. <note>original: "12."; a book size where the sheet is folded into twelve leaves.</note> Geneva, printed and published by Jean de Tournes, 1628. 3 sheets.

*By the same author: Treatise* on the nature of the elements and how they cause wind / rain / lightning and thunder / and how they are useful. Octavo. Printed at Leyden in Holland / by Henrich von Haestens, 1608. 2 sheets.

*By the same author:* Translated from the Dutch into German by **Johann Ernst Burggraffen**, Doctor of Medicine in the County of Katzenelnbogen / at Bad Schwalbach. Octavo. Printed at Frankfurt am Main by Caspar Röthel / published by Wilhelm Fitzer, 1628. Two and a half sheets.

**Durantis (Castore):** *Thesaurus Sanitatis*, that is / a proven Treasury and Golden Jewel of Health / in which all six parts, called the *Non-Natural Things* <note>original: "Res non naturales"; a medical concept from antiquity referring to six environmental and lifestyle factors that affect health: air, food/drink, exercise, sleep, excretion, and emotions.</note> by physicians, pertaining to health / namely: the external air / movements of the body / sleeping and waking / fullness and emptiness <note>Refers to the consumption of food and the excretion of waste.</note> / states of the mind and everything that a person is accustomed to use for their food and drink / are explained in detail.

**On the Utility and Unique Invention of Ore-Leaching and Liquation** <note>original: "Ertz-Beitzung und Seigerung"; Liquation is a metallurgical process where metals are separated by heating them to a temperature where one melts and the other remains solid—specifically used here to separate silver from copper using lead.</note>, namely how one can perform all operations in the liquation of silver-bearing copper using wood instead of coal, and how copper ores can be processed with greater profit than is common; divided into three parts. ### A Precious and Costly Treasure of Euonymus Philiatrus <note>A pseudonym for the famous Swiss naturalist Conrad Gessner (1516–1565).</note>,
in which are contained many secret, high-quality items of medicine, but primarily the nature and properties of **distilled waters and oils**, how one should prepare them, as well as the nature and properties, usefulness, and use of each water and oil. Likewise, how one should prepare many kinds of **wine**, and also how to restore stale wine with the help of distilled waters, spices, and other materials. **Modestin Fachs’s Little Assay-Book** <note>original: "Probier-Büchlein." A standard reference for metallurgy and the testing of ores.</note>, in which a thorough report is given on how to correctly assay all metals and their corresponding metallic ores and drosses, each according to its properties and metal. Likewise, instruction and teaching on the correct assay furnaces, weights, cupels <term>cupel: a shallow, porous vessel used in assaying to separate precious metals from lead and other impurities</term>, and fluxes, including an attached report from Holy Scripture on what weights, alloy-fineness, grains, and stamps the ancient patriarchs, Romans, and Jews in Babylon and Jerusalem used and took for the old coins. Octavo. Leipzig, 1622. Published by Gottfried Grosse.

These were practical manuals for miners and mint-masters to test the purity of metals.</note>, in which that which was perhaps omitted is supplemented, what is obscure is explained, and other matters concerning metallic things are variously introduced and handled; specifically, it discusses the **corporeal mercuries of all metals and their sulfurous components** <note>original: "Mercuriis Metallorum omnium corporeis eorundemque sulphureis." ...[A]postolic [Creed] explained Kabbalistically; also concerning the first Tincture: the Root and *primary matter* <note>original Latin: "materia prima," the formless base substance required for the alchemical Great Work</note>, by Brother Vincentius Koffskius. Also, *The Secret of Philosophical Multiplication in Quality* <note>original Latin: "Arcanum de multiplicatione Philosophica in qualitate"</note>. Quarto. Frankfurt am Main, 1608; and Octavo, in the same place, 1682.

***Benedict Figulus's Golden Olympic Little Garden*** <note>original Latin: "Figuli Benedicti Hortulus Olimpicus Aureolus"</note> **That is;**
A heavenly, golden, hermetic pleasure-garden, planted and cultivated by philosophers old and new; wherein is to be found how the celestial, noble, highly blessed Rose of Sulfur <note>original German: "Schwebelroß," likely a variation of "Schwefel-Rose"</note> and the scarlet tree of the Carbuncle-Stone <note>original German: "Carſunckelſtein," referring to a deep red gem or the red stage of the alchemical process</note> are to be plucked. Quarto. Frankfurt am Main, 1608.

***By the same author, The Golden Hermetic Paradise: Flowing with Nectar and Ambrosia, in the diligent survey of which is offered faithful and eyewitness instruction—how the golden apples of the Hesperides are to be plucked from the blessed philosophical tree: planted by various philosophers, both ancient and modern.*** **Quarto. Frankfurt am Main, 1608.**

***By the same author, The New Olympic and Blessed Rosary.*** <note>original Latin: "Rosarium novum Olympicum & benedictum"</note>
**That is:** A new, blessed Philosophical Rose Garden, in which is shown by the most wise King Solomon, Master Salomon Trismosin <note>The legendary teacher of Paracelsus</note>, Master Trithemius <note>Johannes Trithemius, the polymath abbot and occultist</note>, Doctor Theophrastus <note>Paracelsus</note>, etc., how the blessed golden branch and tincture-treasure is to be plucked and obtained from the unfading Oriental Tree of the Hesperides <note>In alchemy, the "Apples of the Hesperides" symbolize the gold produced by the Stone</note> through the means of Divine Grace. *Part One.* Quarto. Basel, 1608.

***By the same author, the second part of the same work:*** **Containing a book** with 32 chapters by *Lorenzo Ventura of Venice, Doctor of Medicine.* Translated from Latin into German. Quarto. In the same place [Basel], 1608. *See Figulus’s Rosary, 1st Part.*

**Fuchs, Leonhard**, *New Herbal* <note>original German: "Neues Kräuter-Buch"</note>, in which the herbs are depicted from life. Folio. *Basel* 1543.

*Notable Commentaries on the History of Plants* <note>original Latin: "De Historia Stirpium Commentarii insignes"</note>, elaborated with the greatest expense and vigilance, with more than five hundred lifelike images added, never before so skillfully depicted and expressed in the imitation of nature. By Leonhard Fuchs, by far the most famous physician of our age. While many others have traveled to foreign regions at great expense and tireless study—and sometimes at the risk of their lives—to acquire the ability to recognize the substances of "simples" <term>simplicium materiæ</term> <note>Individual medicinal plants or minerals used in their natural state, as opposed to "compounds" mixed by a pharmacist.</note>, you may now come to know this entire subject with the greatest saving of expense and time, and far from all danger, as if in a most pleasant living garden, which you may observe here with great pleasure. Included is a very succinct explanation of the difficult and obscure words occurring here and there in this work. Along with a fourfold index: the first containing the Greek names of the plants; the second, the Latin; the third, those used in the shops of apothecaries <term>seplasiariorum</term> <note>From the Latin for sellers of ointments and perfumes; a common term for druggists.</note> and herbalists; and the fourth containing the German names. Furthermore, by a decree of the most invincible Emperor Charles [V], it is cautioned that no one else may print these commentaries on the history of plants anywhere with impunity, according to the tenor of the privilege previously published by us. *Basel*, at the Isingrin Press, in the year of Christ 1542. Folio.

**Fuller, Thomas**, *Extemporaneous Pharmacopoeia, or a Collection of Prescriptions* <note>original Latin: "Pharmacopoeia extemporanea sive Praescriptorum Sylloge"</note>, in which models of elegant and effective remedies, adapted to almost all medical intentions, are candidly proposed; together with their potencies, method of operation, doses, and accompanying indexes. Sixth, more corrected edition. Octavo. *Amsterdam* 1709. <note>Thomas Fuller was a British physician; "extemporaneous" refers to medicines prepared on the spot for a specific patient rather than pre-mixed "officinal" formulas.</note>

In summary: the entire art of transforming imperfect metals—such as copper, tin, lead, iron, etc.—into perfect ones—namely silver and gold; that is, it contains instructions on how one should make silver and gold, provided with suitable figures and an index, and published by Phileletha. <note>Phileletha is a pseudonym (meaning "Lover of Truth") frequently used by alchemical editors, most notably Eirenaeus Philalethes.</note> Octavo. Erfurt 1710.

# Catalogue of Chemical Books

**Christian Felginer 1718.** With this new title, the publishers wanted to persuade the people (though quite simple-mindedly) that it was a new book, even though it is known to all sensible people that it is the otherwise familiar and inherently good book called: **The Golden Fleece, or Golden Treasury and Chamber of Art** <note>Original German: "Aureum Vellus, oder Güldin Schatz- und Kunst-Kammer." This was one of the most famous alchemical compendiums of the late Renaissance, containing works attributed to Solomon Trismosin.</note> etc., in quarto, formerly printed in Rorschach on Lake Constance in 1598, and in Basel in 1604, in five different tracts. In the year 1708, Christian Liebezeit had it reprinted in Hamburg and brought it into one volume. In the year 1718, Theodor Christoph Felginer wanted to make his wisdom known through the above title, but no one speaks well of him for it. And Dr. Georg Paul Hönn, Counselor and Magistrate in Coburg, etc. etc., has placed such perverse and silly strokes of "wisdom" into his *Lexicon of Fraud* <note>Original German: "Betrugs-Lexicon." Published by Hönn in 1721, this popular work exposed the various tricks and deceptions used by different professions, including dishonest book publishers.</note>, where they truly belong with all the rest.

**Geißler, Friedrich.** *The Excellence of Our Green Lion Panacea,* <term>viridis Panaceæ Leonis</term> <note>The "Green Lion" is a famous alchemical symbol usually representing vitriol or antimony used as a solvent to "devour" metals.</note> taken cabalistically from that enigma of the ancient Sages: "Visit the interior of the Earth; by rectifying, you will find the hidden stone of the true universal medicine," <note>This is a translation of the famous Latin alchemical acronym VITRIOL: "Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem."</note> with figures. Duodecimo. *Nuremberg* 1678.

**Geiger, Malachias.** *The Hypochondriac Microcosm,* <term>Microcosmus Hypochondriacus</term> <note>In the 17th century, "hypochondria" referred specifically to ailments of the upper abdomen (the hypochondrium), which were believed to cause melancholy.</note> or a Treatise on Hypochondriacal Melancholy, with copperplate figures. Quarto. *Munich* 1652.

->Concerning Gemstones.<-

**Boyle, Robert.** *A Specimen on the Origin and Virtues of Gems.* <note>Robert Boyle, though often called the "Father of Chemistry," remained deeply interested in the traditional "virtues" or medicinal/physical powers of precious stones.</note> Duodecimo. *Hamburg* 1673.

**de Boot, Anselmus Boetius.** *The History of Gems and Stones.* Octavo. *Leiden* 1636.

**Evax (King of the Arabs).** *Writing on Gemstones.* <note>Evax is a legendary figure; a poem on the properties of stones was often attributed to him in the Middle Ages and Renaissance.</note> Octavo. *Wittenberg* 1674.

**Nicols, Thomas.** *The Little Book of Gemstones,* or a Description of Precious Stones. Octavo. *Hamburg* 1675.

...collected and described by **Dr. Conrad Gessner** <note>Conrad Gessner (1516–1565) was a legendary Swiss polymath; this likely refers to a German edition of one of his botanical or bibliographic works.</note>, and later expanded by the very learned **Mr. Jacobus Carronus**, and brought into this order: but now translated into German with special diligence. **Dialogue in the Realm of the Dead** between the world-renowned gold-makers, **Count Cajetani** and the famous **Baron von Klettenberg**; the first of whom was hanged in Küstrin on a beam of the common thief’s gallows plated with golden tinsel, wearing a Roman-style habit made of the same material <note>Domenico Manuel Caetano (Count Cajetani) was an alchemist-conman executed in 1709; his execution was intentionally ironic, using "gold" decorations to mock his failed promises to transmute metals.</note>, while the latter was decapitated at the well-known mountain fortress of Königstein <note>Johann Friedrich von Klettenberg was executed in 1720 for failing to produce gold for Augustus the Strong.</note>. 4to <note>Quarto format.</note>. Hamburg 1721.

**Health Remedies**, four curious and universal ones, by which any and all illnesses may be prevented in both women and men, and through which the greater part of them can be cured; or, a detailed description, origin, preparation, use, and effect of **Chinese Tea, Turkish Coffee, Spanish Chocolate, and German Tobacco**. Written by **Theophilo Techno** <note>A pseudonym meaning "Lover of the Craft."</note>. 8vo <note>Octavo, a portable book size.</note>. Augsburg 1699.

**Gilbert, (William)**: *A Treatise, or New Physiology on the Magnet, Magnetic Bodies, and the Great Magnet Earth* <note>Original Latin: "Tractatus sive Phisiologia Nova De Magnete, Magneticisque corporibus & Magno Magnete tellure." This is a foundational work of modern magnetism and electricity.</note>, comprised in six books, in which those things pertaining to this matter are treated and explained most exactly and completely with many arguments and experiments. All now diligently revised and published more correctly than before, expanded and illustrated with figures through the work and study of **Wolfgang Lochmann**. 4to. Frankfurt 1638.

**Glaser, C.**, *The New Medico-Chemical Laboratory* <note>Christophe Glaser (1615–1672) was a royal apothecary and a key figure in 17th-century chemistry.</note>, that is: A newly opened Chemical School of Medicine and Craft, divided into three books. The first presents a brief instruction on all those pie-

...which are required and must be understood for the foundational learning, or theory, of this **noble science**. The second part reveals the common artistic methods and manipulations of the **vegetable, animal, and mineral kingdoms**. The third part comprises, in three distinct sections, **one hundred and nine chemical and secret remedies and curative means**. Translated from French into German by **Johann Marschalck**; with many figures <note>original: "Figuren," referring to illustrations or diagrams</note>. 8vo. Nuremberg 1677.

***By the same author*** <note>original: "Ejusdem," referring here to Nicolas Le Febure</note> **Chemical Guide**, that is, a reliable instruction to the chemical art, wherein a short path and easy manipulations are shown, explaining how one can prepare all kinds of medicines through chemistry. Translated from French into German. With many figures. 12mo. Jena 1710.

***By the same author*** 12mo. Published by Johann Jacob Bauhofer, 1684.

**Glauber Concentrated**. Or, the Core of the **Glauberian Writings**, wherein all unnecessary disputation is omitted, what is useful is condensed, and what was unclear or hidden is made as clear as possible and brought into the form of an easily understandable process. 4to. Breslau 1715.

***By the same author*** the **Rich Treasury and Collection-Box**, or **General Appendix** <note>original: "Appendicis Generali"</note> &c. **Centuries I, II, III, IV, V.** <note>A "Century" in this context refers to a collection of one hundred observations, recipes, or secrets.</note> Along with the interpretation of Solomon regarding the words: **Concerning the fifth essence of metals** <note>original: "De quinta essentia Metallorum." The "fifth essence" or quintessence was the purest, most potent part of a substance.</note>. 8vo. Amsterdam 1660.

***By the same author*** **Mineral Work** <note>original: "Operis Mineralis"</note>, or a description of many artistic and useful metallic labors, wherein it is taught how one shall extract gold from **pebble-stones, quartz, sand, earth**, and other poor types of mountain ores—which otherwise cannot be profitably smelted—using the **Spirit of Salt** <note>original: "Spiritum Salis"; this is an old term for hydrochloric acid, used by Glauber for various metallurgical extractions.</note> and make it corporeal; also how a **panacea** <note>A universal medicine or "cure-all."</note>, or general medicine, may be made from **antimony**, and how such is to be used. Comprised in three parts. 8vo. Frankfurt am Main 1651 & Ibidem <note>In the same place.</note> 1655.

# Catalogue of Chemical Books

**By the same author** <note>original: "Ejusdem," referring here to Johann Rudolf Glauber</note>, the same work in three parts. 8vo. Prague 1705.

**Johann Rudolf Glauber’s Miracle of the World** <note>original: "Miraculum Mundi"</note>, or an extensive description of the wonderful nature, kind, and property of the **greatly powerful Subject** <note>In alchemy, the "Subject" refers to the raw material intended for transformation.</note>, called by the ancients the **universal solvent** <note>original: "Menstruum universale"</note>, or the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Mercurius Philosophorum"</note>; through which plants, animals, and minerals can be very easily transformed into the most healing medicines, and **imperfect metals** <note>Base metals like lead or tin.</note> can be truly changed into stable and **perfect metals** <note>Precious metals like gold and silver.</note>. 8vo. Rothenburg ob der Tauber 1653.

**By the same author**, the same work. 8vo. Hanau 1653.

---------- **2nd Part.** 8vo. Amsterdam 1660.

**By the same author**, the same work with **copperplates** <note>Engraved illustrations.</note>. 8vo. Prague 1704.

**By the same author, Concerning Elijah the Artist** <note>original: "de Elia Artista"</note>, or who **Elijah the Artist** <note>A symbolic figure in alchemy predicted by Paracelsus to appear and reveal the ultimate secrets of nature.</note> might be, and what he will reform or improve in the world when he comes? Namely: the true **Spagyric Medicine** <note>A method of preparing herbal medicines using alchemical techniques like fermentation, distillation, and extraction.</note> of the ancient Egyptian philosophers, which had been lost for more than a thousand years; he shall draw it forth again, renovate it, and magnificently illuminate it through new inventions. 8vo. Amsterdam 1668. See Glauber’s **Miracle of the World**, 2nd Part, Amsterdam edition.

**By the same author, Glauber Concentrated**, or the **Glauberian Laboratory**; containing the specification and valuation of those medicinal and chemical **secrets** <note>original: "Arcanitäten," referring to *arcana* or hidden medicinal preparations.</note> which were prepared one after another over many years in the mentioned laboratory, and which still remain now after the lab work has ceased. Along with all the artistic **furnaces and instruments** used in the laboratory. 8vo. Amsterdam 1668. See Glauber’s **Miracle of the World**, Amsterdam Edition, 2nd Part.

**By the same author, Germany’s Prosperity** <note>original: "des Teutschlands Wohlfahrt"</note>, divided into 6 parts; wherein the First Part deals with the concentration of **wine, grain, and wood**, along with their useful application. In the Second Part, how minerals are to be concentrated by **saltpeter** <note>original: "Nitrum"</note>, and into metallic and bet-

...to transform [other] bodies. In the **Third Part**, concerning the preparation of **saltpeter** <note>Potassium nitrate, essential for both chemistry and the production of gunpowder.</note>. In the **Fourth Part**, concerning the preparation of good, powerful metallic medicinal **confections**, as well as how one should prepare **golden drinks** <note>Potassium silicate or colloidal gold solutions, often called "aurum potabile" or potable gold, believed to be a universal cure.</note>. In the **Fifth Part**, what **alchemy** <term>Alchymia</term> is, and how through it great benefit can be created in all places of Germany, shown as if with figures. In the **Sixth part**, not only is that which was already made known in the fifth part further explained, but also that which is further revealed to be known for the **defense** <term>Defension</term> of the Fatherland. Along with the addition of tangible proof that the **transmutation** <term>Transmutation</term> of lesser metals into better ones through salt and fire is very much true, both **specifically** <note>original: "particulariter"</note> and **universally** <note>original: "Vniversaliter"</note>. With copperplates. Together with a **continuation** to each part. 8vo. Prague 1704.

**Johann Rudolf Glauber’s Annotations** <note>original: "Annotationes"</note> regarding the **Appendix**, which was placed at the end of the fifth part of the *Philosophical Defense*, and treats <note>original: "tractiret"</note> various good, useful, and uncommon **secrets** <note>In this context, "secrets" refers to specialized laboratory recipes or techniques not known to the general public.</note>. 8vo. Prague 1702. *See Glauber’s New Philosophical Furnaces* <note>original: "vide Glauberi furni novi philosophici"</note>.

**By the same author, Treatise on the Signature of Salts, Metals, and Planets** <note>original: "Tractatus de Signatura Salium Metallorum & Planetarum"</note>, that is: Thorough instruction on how, or in what manner, one can very easily learn and calculate—not from books or writings, but purely and alone from their **signature** <note>The "Doctrine of Signatures" was the belief that the physical appearance of a substance (its shape, color, or smell) was a sign from God indicating its medicinal or spiritual purpose.</note>, through a **circle** <note>original: "Circulum"</note> and **quadrant**—the hidden powers, meaning, nature, and properties not only of **salts**, metals, and planets, but also of words and names. 8vo. Prague 1703. *See Glauber’s New Philosophical Furnaces.*

**By the same author, A Little Book of Dialogues** <note>original: "Libellus Dialogorum"</note>, or a little conversation-book between several lovers of **Hermetic medicine** <note>Medicine based on the mystical and alchemical traditions attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, emphasizing the spiritual purity of substances.</note> concerning the **universal tincture** <note>original: "Tincturam Vniversalem," another name for the Philosopher's Stone or a liquid form of it capable of transmuting any substance or curing any disease.</note>. 8vo. Prague 1703. Amsterdam 1663. *See Glauber’s New Philosophical Furnaces.*

<meta>This page continues the bibliographic catalog of Johann Rudolf Glauber's works, specifically focusing on his later appendices and treatises concerning salts, universal medicine, and the metallurgical "three principles" (sulfur, mercury, and salt).</meta>

### Catalogue of Chemical Books

Glauber, *Johann Rudolf*: Appendix to the 7th Part of his **Spagyric Dispensary** <note>original: "Spagyrischen Apothecken"; "Spagyric" refers to alchemical medicine produced by separating and recombining the constituents of a substance.</note>, in which is treated the further use of our secret **sal ammoniac** <note>original: "Salis armoniaci"; ammonium chloride, a vital reagent in alchemical laboratories for dissolving metals.</note> in the improvement of lesser metals, and especially concerning the useful extraction or parting of gold and silver from tin. Along with an added instruction on how, by means of the **mercurial water of tin** <note>original: "per aquam mercurialem jovis"; in alchemy, "Jupiter" refers to the metal tin.</note>, not only gold, but also **iron** <note>original: "martè"</note> & **copper** <note>original: "venerè"</note>, etc., may be extracted in **abundance** <note>original: "in copia"</note>. 8vo. *Amsterdam* 1668.

*By the same author: **A Treatise on the Nature of Salts**, or a clear description, declaring with a perfect explanation the nature, properties, and uses of salts, etc. With a most firm demonstration that (after God and the Sun) salt is the unique principle, and the sole origin, propagation, and unique increase of all things, from which the greatest treasure of the whole world and the greatest riches may be unearthed. Likewise, a small and compendious treatise on the **signature** <term>Signatura</term> <note>The "doctrine of signatures" was the belief that the physical appearance of a substance (its shape or color) revealed its divinely intended medicinal or alchemical use.</note> of salts, metals, and planets.* 8vo. *Amsterdam* 1659.

*By the same author: **A Treatise on the Three Principles of Metals**, namely sulfur, mercury, and the **Salt of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Sale philosophorum"; these three principles were believed to be the fundamental components of all metals.</note>.* 8vo. *Ibid.* 1667.

*By the same author: **A Treatise on Universal Medicine**, or the **True Potable Gold** <note>original: "auro potabili vero"</note>*, or a detailed description of a true universal medicine, as well as its wonderful great power and effect which it demonstrates upon vegetables, animals, and minerals. 8vo. Amsterdam 1657.

By the same author: **Concerning the Three Beginnings of Metals**, namely sulfur, mercury, and the **Salt of the Wise** <note>original: "Saltz der Weisen"</note>, how the same may be usefully employed in medicine, **alchemy** <term>Alchymia</term>, and other secondary arts. 8vo. *Ibid.* 1666. *See Glauber’s Universal Medicine.*
